````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
44	
45	
46	class BaseTimeSeries(QTable):
47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78	
79	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
80	                                 "as the first column{} but found '{}'"
81	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural, self.colnames[0]))
82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
84	                    and self._required_columns == self.colnames[:len(self._required_columns)]):
85	                self._required_columns_relax = False
86	
87	    @contextmanager
88	    def _delay_required_column_checks(self):
89	        self._required_columns_enabled = False
90	        yield
````

````text
[note | m=284 | _init_from_list]
Good progress! Most TimeSeries tests pass now. The remaining failures are:

1. **`test_add_column`**: The test calls `ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')` but `add_column` expects a column object, not a plain list. I need to update the test to create a Column first, or fix the implementation.

2. **BinnedTimeSeries**: Several failures — the `_init_from_list` error says `missing 3 required positional arguments: 'dtype', 'n_cols', and 'copy'`. In astropy 3.2, `Table._init_from_list` takes `(self, cols, names, dtype, n_cols, copy)`. Let me check the actual signature. Also, the `Time - Time` operation might not produce a `TimeDelta` in the expected way.

Let me check the `_init_from_list` signature and the `Time.__sub__` behavior.
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
44	
45	
46	class BaseTimeSeries(QTable):
47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78	
79	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
80	                                 "as the first column{} but found '{}'"
81	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural, self.colnames[0]))
82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
84	                    and self._required_columns == self.colnames[:len(self._required_columns)]):
85	                self._required_columns_relax = False
86	
87	    @contextmanager
88	    def _delay_required_column_checks(self):
89	        self._required_columns_enabled = False
90	        yield
````

````text
[note | m=284 | _init_from_list]
Good progress! Most TimeSeries tests pass now. The remaining failures are:

1. **`test_add_column`**: The test calls `ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')` but `add_column` expects a column object, not a plain list. I need to update the test to create a Column first, or fix the implementation.

2. **BinnedTimeSeries**: Several failures — the `_init_from_list` error says `missing 3 required positional arguments: 'dtype', 'n_cols', and 'copy'`. In astropy 3.2, `Table._init_from_list` takes `(self, cols, names, dtype, n_cols, copy)`. Let me check the actual signature. Also, the `Time - Time` operation might not produce a `TimeDelta` in the expected way.

Let me check the `_init_from_list` signature and the `Time.__sub__` behavior.
````

````text
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.

26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper

39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
````

````text
# Issue

Astropy's units module lacked support for the furlong, an imperial unit of length (660 feet / 201.168 m). This prevented conversions to/from furlong and use of derived units such as velocity in furlong/fortnight, which the PR body specifically calls out as a goal.

# Your task

The repository is checked out at `/testbed`. Investigate the issue above and make the necessary source changes to address it. Save all changes to /testbed.
````

````text
```

---

### 3. How TimeSeries Handles `to_pandas`

**File: `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/sampled.py`**

`TimeSeries` has its own `to_pandas()` at line 306:

```python
def to_pandas(self):
    return Table(self).to_pandas(index='time')
```

It casts the TimeSeries to a plain `Table` (which copies the `primary_key` from the source) and then explicitly sets `index='time'`. This means the `'time'` column is always the DataFrame index, regardless of what the primary key is.

Index setup happens lazily in `add_column` and `add_columns` overrides (lines 258-276):

```python
def add_column(self, *args, **kwargs):
    result = super().add_column(*args, **kwargs)
    if len(self.indices) == 0 and 'time' in self.colnames:
        self.add_index('time')
    return result
```

When the `'time'` column is first added, an index is automatically created, making it the primary key.

---

### 4. How BinnedTimeSeries Handles Indexes

**File: `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/binned.py`**

`BinnedTimeSeries` does **not** have its own `to_pandas()` method. It inherits from `BaseTimeSeries -> QTable -> Table`, so it relies on the base `Table.to_pandas()`.

During `__init__` (line 184 of binned.py):

```python
self.add_index('time_bin_start')
```

This adds `time_bin_start` as the primary key. Therefore, calling `binned_ts.to_pandas()` with no arguments will auto-detect this primary key and use `time_bin_start` as the DataFrame index.

The class has `_required_columns = ['time_bin_start', 'time_bin_size']` enforced by the `autocheck_required_columns` decorator in `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/core.py` (line 19). This decorator wraps column-modification methods (`add_column`, `add_columns`, `remove_column`, etc.) to check that the required columns are present after each operation.

---

### 5. Recent Changes to Index Handling in `to_pandas`

Two critical bugfixes in the current codebase:

**Commit `5e55bcbd5` (March 2021) -- "Fix table.to_pandas incorrect behavior with masked data"**

Added the code block that sets `v.index = idx` on Series values (lines 3749-3752). Previously, when a table had masked integer columns and an index, the masked integer column was converted to a pandas `Series` with its own default `RangeIndex`, which conflicted with the desired DataFrame index. Fixes issue #11432.

**Commit `c37e554e9` (July 2021) -- "Fix problem in to_pandas() introduced in pandas 1.3.0"**

Added the explicit `df.index.name = idx.info.name` assignment (lines 3755-3758). In pandas 1.3.0, the DataFrame constructor stopped automatically preserving the index name from a Series used as the index parameter. This fix ensures the index name is always carried over.

---
````

````text
# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst

import pytest
import numpy as np

from astropy import units as u
from astropy.time import Time, TimeDelta
from astropy.timeseries import TimeSeries, BinnedTimeSeries
from astropy.table import QTable


class TestTimeSeries:
    """Tests for the TimeSeries class."""

    def setup_method(self, method):
        self.time = Time([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], format='mjd')
        self.data = {'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]}

    def test_initialization_with_time(self):
        """Test that TimeSeries can be initialized with time as a keyword."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts, TimeSeries)
        assert len(ts) == 5
        assert ts.colnames == ['time']
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_initialization_with_time_and_data(self):
        """Test initialization with time and data."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        assert ts.colnames == ['time', 'flux']
        assert len(ts) == 5
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts['flux'], self.data['flux'])

    def test_initialization_time_position(self):
        """Test that time is always the first column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_list(self):
        """Test initialization with time as a list."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=[1, 2, 3, 4, 5], data=self.data)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        assert len(ts) == 5

    def test_initialization_without_time_raises_error(self):
        """Test that initializing without time raises an error."""
        with pytest.raises(TypeError) as exc:
            TimeSeries(data=self.data)
        assert 'time' in str(exc.value)

    def test_time_property(self):
        """Test the time property."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_fold(self):
        """Test folding a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        period = 2.0 * u.day
        midpoint_epoch = Time(2, format='mjd')

        folded = ts.fold(period=period, midpoint_epoch=midpoint_epoch)
        assert isinstance(folded, TimeSeries)
        assert len(folded) == len(ts)

        # Check that folded times are within [0, period)
        delta = (folded.time - midpoint_epoch)
        for d in delta.sec * u.s:
            assert 0 <= (d % period.to(u.s)).value < period.to(u.s).value

    def test_fold_requires_period(self):
        """Test that fold requires a period."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        with pytest.raises(ValueError) as exc:
            ts.fold()
        assert 'period' in str(exc.value)

    def test_copy(self):
        """Test copying a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts_copy = ts.copy()
        assert isinstance(ts_copy, TimeSeries)
        assert ts_copy.colnames == ts.colnames
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts_copy.time.mjd, ts.time.mjd)

    def test_getitem_keeps_time_first(self):
        """Test that getting columns keeps time first."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        subset = ts['flux', 'temp']
        assert subset.colnames[0] == 'time'
        assert 'flux' in subset.colnames
        assert 'temp' in subset.colnames

    def test_add_column(self):
        """Test adding a column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')
        assert 'newcol' in ts.colnames
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_object_in_data(self):
        """Test that time in data as Time object works."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)

    def test_read_method_exists(self):
        """Test that read class method exists."""
        assert hasattr(TimeSeries, 'read')
````

````text
Here is the comprehensive report of my findings.

---

## 1. Where Imperial Units Are Defined

The primary definition file is:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`** (full file, 138 lines)

This file contains all imperial unit definitions, organized by physical category. It uses the `def_unit` factory function (imported from `core.py`) and a `_ns = globals()` namespace trick to inject the units into the module's own namespace. Units are not active by default -- they must be enabled via `imperial.enable()` or the context manager `u.add_enabled_units(imperial)`.

The `__init__.py` at `/testbed/astropy/units/__init__.py` (line 34) confirms this: `set_enabled_units([si, cgs, astrophys])` is called, and notably imperial is NOT included. A comment on line 31-32 states: "Enable the set of default units. This notably does *not* include Imperial units."

---

## 2. How Existing Imperial Length Units Are Defined (Exact Code Patterns)

All length units are in `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`, lines 22-32:

```python
def_unit(['inch'], 2.54 * si.cm, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International inch")
def_unit(['ft', 'foot'], 12 * inch, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International foot")
def_unit(['yd', 'yard'], 3 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International yard")
def_unit(['mi', 'mile'], 5280 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International mile")
def_unit(['nmi', 'nauticalmile', 'NM'], 1852 * si.m, namespace=_ns,
         doc="Nautical mile")
```

The pattern is:
- **First argument**: a list of strings. The first element is the canonical short name (e.g. `'ft'`), and subsequent elements are aliases (e.g. `'foot'`).
- **Second argument**: the unit's definition relative to another unit. These are built as a chain: `inch` is defined in terms of `si.cm`, `ft` in terms of `inch`, `yd` in terms of `ft`, `mi` in terms of `ft`. The nautical mile (`nmi`) is defined directly from `si.m`.
- **`namespace=_ns`**: injects the unit into the module's `globals()` so it can be accessed as `imperial.mile`, `imperial.ft`, etc.
- **`doc=...`**: human-readable description.

Other imperial unit categories follow the same pattern:
- Areas: line 38 (`acre`)
- Volumes: lines 45-58 (`gallon`, `quart`, `pint`, `cup`, `fluid_oz`, `tablespoon`, `teaspoon`)
- Mass: lines 66-71 (`ounce`, `pound`, `ton`)
- Speed: line 77 (`knot`)
- Energy: lines 84-90 (`BTU`, `cal`, `kcal`)
- Power: line 97 (`horsepower`)
- Temperature: line 104 (`deg_F`)

The `def_unit` factory function itself is defined at `/testbed/astropy/units/core.py`, line 2181. Its signature:
```python
def def_unit(s, represents=None, register=None, doc=None,
             format=None, prefixes=False, exclude_prefixes=[],
             namespace=None):
```

The `enable()` function is defined at `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`, lines 124-137:
```python
def enable():
    """Enable Imperial units so they appear in results of
    `~astropy.units.UnitBase.find_equivalent_units` and
    `~astropy.units.UnitBase.compose`.
    This may be used with the ``with`` statement to enable Imperial
    units only temporarily."""
    from .core import add_enabled_units
    import inspect
    return add_enabled_units(inspect.getmodule(enable))
```

Conversion equivalence for a furlong: 1 furlong = 660 ft (i.e., `660 * ft` following the existing chain pattern), or equivalently `201.168 * si.m`.

---

## 3. Whether "furlong" Is Partially Referenced Anywhere Already

Only **one** reference exists in the entire codebase:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, line 440**:
```python
def test_vounit_implicit_custom():
    x = u.Unit("furlong/week", format="vounit")
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.scale == 1e-15
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.bases[0].name == 'urlong'
```

This is a VOUnit format test, not an imperial units test. The test verifies how the VOUnit parser handles an unknown/custom unit string. The string `"furlong/week"` is parsed as an implicit custom unit where `furlong` gets decomposed into a femto (`f`) prefix on an unknown `urlong` base (hence `scale == 1e-15` and `name == 'urlong'`). This is a quirk of the parser, not an actual furlong definition -- it mistakenly interprets the `f` in `furlong` as the femto SI prefix. So there is no real furlong unit definition anywhere in the codebase.

---

## 4. Test Files That Test Imperial Units

Three test files reference and test imperial units:

### a) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_units.py`, lines 31-38
Tests the "Getting Started" workflow with imperial units: enables imperial units, converts cm/s to mile/hour, and tests `_get_converter`.

### b) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity.py`, lines 401-411
`test_complicated_operation`: imports imperial, creates a distance (15 m) and time (11 s), then converts velocity to `imperial.mile / u.hour`. Asserts the value is approximately 3.05037.

Here's a summary of the changes made to add furlong support:

## Changes

### 1. `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py` (line 31-32)
Added the furlong unit definition in the LENGTH section, between mile and nautical mile:

```python
def_unit(['fur', 'furlong'], 660 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International furlong")
```

- **Short name:** `fur` (primary), **long name:** `furlong` (alias)
- **Definition:** 660 × foot (consistent with the chain-pattern used by other imperial length units)
- **Equivalences:** 1 furlong = 201.168 m = 660 ft = 1/8 mile

### 2. `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_equivalencies.py` (lines 400-405)
Updated the `test_equivalent_units2` test to include `imperial.fur` in the expected set of imperial units returned by `find_equivalent_units` when imperial units are enabled.

- Basic conversions: furlong ↔ meters, furlong ↔ feet ✓
- Chain equivalence: 8 furlongs = 1 mile ✓
- Derived units: furlong/fortnight (velocity) correctly converts to m/s and mi/h ✓
- Both `fur` and `furlong` aliases work ✓
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
44

46	class BaseTimeSeries(QTable):
47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78	
79	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
80	                                 "as the first column{} but found '{}'"
81	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural, self.colnames[0]))
82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
84	                    and self._required_columns == self.colnames[:len(self._required_columns)]):
85	                self._required_columns_relax = False
````

````text
Conversion equivalence for a furlong: 1 furlong = 660 ft (i.e., `660 * ft` following the existing chain pattern), or equivalently `201.168 * si.m`.

---

## 3. Whether "furlong" Is Partially Referenced Anywhere Already

Only **one** reference exists in the entire codebase:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, line 440**:
```python
def test_vounit_implicit_custom():
    x = u.Unit("furlong/week", format="vounit")
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.scale == 1e-15
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.bases[0].name == 'urlong'
```

This is a VOUnit format test, not an imperial units test. The test verifies how the VOUnit parser handles an unknown/custom unit string. The string `"furlong/week"` is parsed as an implicit custom unit where `furlong` gets decomposed into a femto (`f`) prefix on an unknown `urlong` base (hence `scale == 1e-15` and `name == 'urlong'`). This is a quirk of the parser, not an actual furlong definition -- it mistakenly interprets the `f` in `furlong` as the femto SI prefix. So there is no real furlong unit definition anywhere in the codebase.

---

## 4. Test Files That Test Imperial Units

Three test files reference and test imperial units:

### a) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_units.py`, lines 31-38
Tests the "Getting Started" workflow with imperial units: enables imperial units, converts cm/s to mile/hour, and tests `_get_converter`.

### b) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity.py`, lines 401-411
`test_complicated_operation`: imports imperial, creates a distance (15 m) and time (11 s), then converts velocity to `imperial.mile / u.hour`. Asserts the value is approximately 3.05037.

### c) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_equivalencies.py`, lines 390-412, 514-518
- `test_equivalent_units2` (line 390): Enables imperial units and verifies they appear in `find_equivalent_units(u.spectral())` results, explicitly checking `imperial.BTU`, `imperial.cal`, `imperial.ft`, `imperial.inch`, `imperial.kcal`, `imperial.mi`, `imperial.yd`, and `imperial.nmi` (lines 401-405).
- `test_temperature` (line 514): Uses `imperial.deg_F` to test temperature conversions to Fahrenheit.

### d) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity_ufuncs.py`, line 228
Defines an ad-hoc `inch` unit (`u.Unit(0.0254 * u.m)`) for testing `divmod` -- but this does not reference the imperial module.

### e) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, lines 439-442
`test_vounit_implicit_custom`: Contains the `"furlong/week"` string used as a VOUnit-format custom unit test (not actually imperial-related).

There is **no** dedicated `test_imperial.py` file in `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/`.

[summary | m=114 | astropy/units/imperial.py]
Here's a summary of the changes made to add furlong support:

## Changes

### 1. `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py` (line 31-32)
Added the furlong unit definition in the LENGTH section, between mile and nautical mile:

```python
def_unit(['fur', 'furlong'], 660 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International furlong")
```

- **Short name:** `fur` (primary), **long name:** `furlong` (alias)
- **Definition:** 660 × foot (consistent with the chain-pattern used by other imperial length units)
- **Equivalences:** 1 furlong = 201.168 m = 660 ft = 1/8 mile

### 2. `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_equivalencies.py` (lines 400-405)
Updated the `test_equivalent_units2` test to include `imperial.fur` in the expected set of imperial units returned by `find_equivalent_units` when imperial units are enabled.

## Verified
- Basic conversions: furlong ↔ meters, furlong ↔ feet ✓
- Chain equivalence: 8 furlongs = 1 mile ✓
- Derived units: furlong/fortnight (velocity) correctly converts to m/s and mi/h ✓
- Both `fur` and `furlong` aliases work ✓
- Existing tests pass (the one pre-existing failure in `test_numeric_converters` is an unrelated numpy repr formatting issue) ✓
````

````text
+                        terminal_width = int(cols)

+                    pass
+        return terminal_width
````

````text
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
````

````text
# Issue

The ProgressBar class in astropy/utils/console.py had a display corruption issue when the terminal window was resized during progress bar rendering. The ANSI escape sequences and progress bar characters would become misaligned, making the output unreadable. This was a user-facing usability problem on any platform where the terminal could be resized while a long-running progress operation was in progress.

# Your task

The repository is checked out at `/testbed`. Investigate the issue above and make the necessary source changes to address it. Save all changes to /testbed.

[summary | m=58 | astropy/utils/console.py]
The diff looks clean and correct. Here's a summary of the changes made to fix the terminal resize corruption issue in `ProgressBar`:

### Changes to `/testbed/astropy/utils/console.py`

**1. Extracted `_get_terminal_width()` method** — Previously, terminal width detection was done once inline in `__init__` using only `stty size` on Linux. The new method:

- Uses `fcntl.ioctl` with `TIOCGWINSZ` (the POSIX-standard way to get terminal size) as the primary method — this is much faster since it doesn't spawn a subprocess.
- Falls back to `stty size` on Linux if `fcntl`/`termios` aren't available.
- Returns 78 as a sensible default if all methods fail.

**2. Dynamic width recalculation in `update()`** — Each call to `update()` now re-fetches `_terminal_width` and recalculates `_bar_length`. This means the progress bar adapts to terminal resizes immediately on the next update tick.

**3. Minimum bar length guard** — Added `if self._bar_length < 10: self._bar_length = 10` to prevent degenerate rendering on very narrow terminals.

**4. ANSI `\033[K` clear-to-end-of-line** — After writing the progress bar content, emits `\033[K` to clear any stale characters that could remain from a previously longer line (e.g., when the terminal is made narrower after being wider).

**5. Initialization robustness** — `_bar_length` and `_current_value` are now always set in `__init__`, even in silent (non-tty) mode, so code that accesses these attributes (like `ProgressBar.map`) doesn't break.
````

````text
21	class TimeSeries(QTable):
22	    """
23	    A class to represent time series data.
24	
25	    The `TimeSeries` class is a subclass of `~astropy.table.QTable` and
26	    provides a convenient way to work with time series data. It requires a
27	    ``time`` column which is used as the time coordinate.
28	
29	    The data can be provided in the same way as for `~astropy.table.QTable`,
30	    with the addition of a ``time`` keyword argument that can be used to
31	    specify the time values.
32	
33	    Parameters
34	    ----------
35	    data : numpy ndarray, dict, list, Table, or table-like object, optional
36	        Data to initialize table.
37	    time : `~astropy.time.Time` or list or numpy.ndarray
38	        The time values for the time series. This can be either an
39	        `~astropy.time.Time` object or a value that can be passed to
40	        `~astropy.time.Time` to create one.
41	    **kwargs : dict, optional
42	        Additional keyword arguments are passed to `~astropy.table.QTable`.
43	    """
44	
45	    _time_attr = 'time'
46	
47	    def __init__(self, data=None, *, time=None, **kwargs):
48	
49	        super().__init__(data, **kwargs)
50	
51	        if time is not None:
52	            if not isinstance(time, Time):
53	                time = Time(time)
54	            self.add_column(time, name=TIME_COLUMN, index=0)
55	
56	        # Ensure time column is present
57	        if TIME_COLUMN not in self.colnames:
58	            raise TypeError("'time' has not been specified, and the "
59	                            "input data does not include a 'time' column")
60	
61	        # Make time column a Time object if it isn't already
62	        if not isinstance(self[TIME_COLUMN], Time):
63	            self[TIME_COLUMN] = Time(self[TIME_COLUMN])
64	
65	    @property
66	    def time(self):
67	        """
68	        The time values as a `~astropy.time.Time` object.
69	        """
70	        return self[TIME_COLUMN]
71	
72	    def add_column(self, *args, **kwargs):
73	        """
74	        Add a column to the time series, but ensure that the ``time`` column
75	        can never be removed and is always the first column.
76	        """
77	        super().add_column(*args, **kwargs)
78	        if TIME_COLUMN in self.colnames:
79	            if self.colnames.index(TIME_COLUMN) != 0:
80	                new_order = [TIME_COLUMN] + [name for name in self.colnames
81	                                             if name != TIME_COLUMN]
82	                self._init_from_list([self[name] for name in new_order],
83	                                     names=new_order)
84

86	        """
87	        Add columns to the time series, but ensure that the ``time`` column

90	        super().add_columns(*args, **kwargs)
91	        if TIME_COLUMN in self.colnames:

102	        Parameters

106	        midpoint_epoch : `~astropy.time.Time`

152	    def fold(self, period=None, midpoint_epoch=None):
153	        """
154	        Return a new `TimeSeries` folded with a period and midpoint epoch.
155	
156	        Parameters
157	        ----------
158	        period : `~astropy.units.Quantity`
159	            The period on which to fold the time series.
160	        midpoint_epoch : `~astropy.time.Time`
161	            The midpoint epoch to use for the fold.
162	
163	        Returns
164	        -------
165	        folded_timeseries : `TimeSeries`
166	            The folded time series object.
167	        """
168	        if period is None:
169	            raise ValueError("period is required to fold a time series")
170	
171	        if midpoint_epoch is None:
172	            midpoint_epoch = Time(0, format='jd', scale=self.time.scale)
173	
174	        period = Quantity(period)
175	
176	        new_ts = self.copy()
177	
178	        # Compute the folded time
179	        folded_time = self.time - midpoint_epoch
180	        folded_time = (folded_time.sec * u.s) % period.to(u.s)
181	
182	        new_ts[TIME_COLUMN] = Time(midpoint_epoch + folded_time,
183	                                   format='jd', scale=self.time.scale)
184	
185	        return new_ts

187	    def __getitem__(self, item):
188	        if self._is_list_or_tuple_of_str(item):
189	            if TIME_COLUMN not in item:
190	                item = [TIME_COLUMN] + list(item)
191	        out = super().__getitem__(item)
192	        if isinstance(out, Table) and not isinstance(out, TimeSeries):
193	            out = out.__class__.__new__(TimeSeries)
194	            out._init_from_list(out.columns.values())
195	        return out
196	
197	    def _is_list_or_tuple_of_str(self, item):
198	        """Check if item is a list or tuple of strings."""
199	        return (isinstance(item, (list, tuple)) and
200	                all(isinstance(x, str) for x in item))
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls

47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78

82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
````

````text
Here's a summary of all changes made:

## Changes Summary

### New `astropy/timeseries/` sub-package created:

| File | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `astropy/timeseries/__init__.py` | Module init exporting `TimeSeries` and `BinnedTimeSeries` |
| `astropy/timeseries/core.py` | Core implementation with both classes |
| `astropy/timeseries/setup_package.py` | Build system integration |
| `astropy/timeseries/tests/__init__.py` | Test package init |
| `astropy/timeseries/tests/test_timeseries.py` | 23 comprehensive tests |

**`TimeSeries`** (subclasses `QTable`):
- Flexible `time=` keyword initializer (accepts `Time` objects, lists, arrays)
- `time` property, `fold()` method, column management that keeps time first
- `read()` classmethod for file I/O

**`BinnedTimeSeries`** (subclasses `QTable`):
- Accepts `time_bin_start`, `time_bin_end`, `time_bin_size` combinations
- Auto-computes missing bin information
- `time_bin_center`, `time_bin_start/end/size` properties

### Compatibility fixes (required for numpy 1.23+):

| File | Fix |
|------|-----|
| `astropy/table/_np_utils.pyx` | `np.int` → `int` (+ regenerated `.c`/`.so`) |
| `astropy/io/ascii/core.py` | `np.int/float/str/bool` → builtins |
| `astropy/io/ascii/html.py` | `np.int/float/str/unicode` → builtins |

All **23 tests pass**. The pre-existing `test_convert_overflow[False]` failure is unrelated (Python 3.11 + numpy 1.23 compatibility with old astropy 3.2 C parser).
````

````text
Here is the comprehensive report of my findings.

---

## 1. Where Imperial Units Are Defined

The primary definition file is:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`** (full file, 138 lines)

This file contains all imperial unit definitions, organized by physical category. It uses the `def_unit` factory function (imported from `core.py`) and a `_ns = globals()` namespace trick to inject the units into the module's own namespace. Units are not active by default -- they must be enabled via `imperial.enable()` or the context manager `u.add_enabled_units(imperial)`.

The `__init__.py` at `/testbed/astropy/units/__init__.py` (line 34) confirms this: `set_enabled_units([si, cgs, astrophys])` is called, and notably imperial is NOT included. A comment on line 31-32 states: "Enable the set of default units. This notably does *not* include Imperial units."

---

## 2. How Existing Imperial Length Units Are Defined (Exact Code Patterns)

All length units are in `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`, lines 22-32:

```python
def_unit(['inch'], 2.54 * si.cm, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International inch")
def_unit(['ft', 'foot'], 12 * inch, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International foot")
def_unit(['yd', 'yard'], 3 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International yard")
def_unit(['mi', 'mile'], 5280 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International mile")
def_unit(['nmi', 'nauticalmile', 'NM'], 1852 * si.m, namespace=_ns,
         doc="Nautical mile")
```

The pattern is:
- **First argument**: a list of strings. The first element is the canonical short name (e.g. `'ft'`), and subsequent elements are aliases (e.g. `'foot'`).
- **Second argument**: the unit's definition relative to another unit. These are built as a chain: `inch` is defined in terms of `si.cm`, `ft` in terms of `inch`, `yd` in terms of `ft`, `mi` in terms of `ft`. The nautical mile (`nmi`) is defined directly from `si.m`.
- **`namespace=_ns`**: injects the unit into the module's `globals()` so it can be accessed as `imperial.mile`, `imperial.ft`, etc.
- **`doc=...`**: human-readable description.

Other imperial unit categories follow the same pattern:
- Areas: line 38 (`acre`)
- Volumes: lines 45-58 (`gallon`, `quart`, `pint`, `cup`, `fluid_oz`, `tablespoon`, `teaspoon`)
- Mass: lines 66-71 (`ounce`, `pound`, `ton`)
- Speed: line 77 (`knot`)
- Energy: lines 84-90 (`BTU`, `cal`, `kcal`)
- Power: line 97 (`horsepower`)
- Temperature: line 104 (`deg_F`)

The `def_unit` factory function itself is defined at `/testbed/astropy/units/core.py`, line 2181. Its signature:
```python
def def_unit(s, represents=None, register=None, doc=None,
             format=None, prefixes=False, exclude_prefixes=[],
             namespace=None):
```

The `enable()` function is defined at `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py`, lines 124-137:
```python
def enable():
    """Enable Imperial units so they appear in results of
    `~astropy.units.UnitBase.find_equivalent_units` and
    `~astropy.units.UnitBase.compose`.
    This may be used with the ``with`` statement to enable Imperial
    units only temporarily."""
    from .core import add_enabled_units
    import inspect
    return add_enabled_units(inspect.getmodule(enable))
```

Conversion equivalence for a furlong: 1 furlong = 660 ft (i.e., `660 * ft` following the existing chain pattern), or equivalently `201.168 * si.m`.

---

## 3. Whether "furlong" Is Partially Referenced Anywhere Already

Only **one** reference exists in the entire codebase:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, line 440**:
```python
def test_vounit_implicit_custom():
    x = u.Unit("furlong/week", format="vounit")
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.scale == 1e-15
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.bases[0].name == 'urlong'
```

This is a VOUnit format test, not an imperial units test. The test verifies how the VOUnit parser handles an unknown/custom unit string. The string `"furlong/week"` is parsed as an implicit custom unit where `furlong` gets decomposed into a femto (`f`) prefix on an unknown `urlong` base (hence `scale == 1e-15` and `name == 'urlong'`). This is a quirk of the parser, not an actual furlong definition -- it mistakenly interprets the `f` in `furlong` as the femto SI prefix. So there is no real furlong unit definition anywhere in the codebase.

---

## 4. Test Files That Test Imperial Units

Three test files reference and test imperial units:

### a) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_units.py`, lines 31-38
Tests the "Getting Started" workflow with imperial units: enables imperial units, converts cm/s to mile/hour, and tests `_get_converter`.

### b) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity.py`, lines 401-411
`test_complicated_operation`: imports imperial, creates a distance (15 m) and time (11 s), then converts velocity to `imperial.mile / u.hour`. Asserts the value is approximately 3.05037.
````

````text
# Issue

The ProgressBar class in astropy/utils/console.py had a display corruption issue when the terminal window was resized during progress bar rendering. The ANSI escape sequences and progress bar characters would become misaligned, making the output unreadable. This was a user-facing usability problem on any platform where the terminal could be resized while a long-running progress operation was in progress.

# Your task

The repository is checked out at `/testbed`. Investigate the issue above and make the necessary source changes to address it. Save all changes to /testbed.

+    def _get_terminal_width(self):

+        try:

+            # more reliable than spawning a subprocess.
+            import fcntl
+            import termios
+            import struct
+            s = struct.pack('HHHH', 0, 0, 0, 0)
+            fd_stdout = sys.stdout.fileno()
+            res = fcntl.ioctl(fd_stdout, termios.TIOCGWINSZ, s)
+            rows, cols = struct.unpack('HHHH', res)[:2]
+            if cols > 0:
+                terminal_width = int(cols)
+        except (ImportError, IOError, AttributeError):

+                        close_fds=True)
+                    stdout, stderr = p.communicate()
+                    parts = stdout.split()
+                    if len(parts) == 2:
+                        rows, cols = parts
+                        terminal_width = int(cols)
+                except (OSError, ValueError):
+                    pass
+        return terminal_width

[summary | m=58 | astropy/utils/console.py]
The diff looks clean and correct. Here's a summary of the changes made to fix the terminal resize corruption issue in `ProgressBar`:

### Changes to `/testbed/astropy/utils/console.py`

**1. Extracted `_get_terminal_width()` method** — Previously, terminal width detection was done once inline in `__init__` using only `stty size` on Linux. The new method:

- Uses `fcntl.ioctl` with `TIOCGWINSZ` (the POSIX-standard way to get terminal size) as the primary method — this is much faster since it doesn't spawn a subprocess.
- Falls back to `stty size` on Linux if `fcntl`/`termios` aren't available.
- Returns 78 as a sensible default if all methods fail.

**2. Dynamic width recalculation in `update()`** — Each call to `update()` now re-fetches `_terminal_width` and recalculates `_bar_length`. This means the progress bar adapts to terminal resizes immediately on the next update tick.

**3. Minimum bar length guard** — Added `if self._bar_length < 10: self._bar_length = 10` to prevent degenerate rendering on very narrow terminals.

**4. ANSI `\033[K` clear-to-end-of-line** — After writing the progress bar content, emits `\033[K` to clear any stale characters that could remain from a previously longer line (e.g., when the terminal is made narrower after being wider).

**5. Initialization robustness** — `_bar_length` and `_current_value` are now always set in `__init__`, even in silent (non-tty) mode, so code that accesses these attributes (like `ProgressBar.map`) doesn't break.
````

````text
152	    def fold(self, period=None, midpoint_epoch=None):
153	        """
154	        Return a new `TimeSeries` folded with a period and midpoint epoch.
155	
156	        Parameters
157	        ----------
158	        period : `~astropy.units.Quantity`
159	            The period on which to fold the time series.
160	        midpoint_epoch : `~astropy.time.Time`
161	            The midpoint epoch to use for the fold.
162	
163	        Returns
164	        -------
165	        folded_timeseries : `TimeSeries`
166	            The folded time series object.
167	        """
168	        if period is None:
169	            raise ValueError("period is required to fold a time series")
170	
171	        if midpoint_epoch is None:
172	            midpoint_epoch = Time(0, format='jd', scale=self.time.scale)
173	
174	        period = Quantity(period)
175	
176	        new_ts = self.copy()
177	
178	        # Compute the folded time
179	        folded_time = self.time - midpoint_epoch
180	        folded_time = (folded_time.sec * u.s) % period.to(u.s)
181	
182	        new_ts[TIME_COLUMN] = Time(midpoint_epoch + folded_time,
183	                                   format='jd', scale=self.time.scale)
184	
185	        return new_ts

191	        out = super().__getitem__(item)

193	            out = out.__class__.__new__(TimeSeries)
````

````text
[summary | m=58 | astropy/utils/console.py]
The diff looks clean and correct. Here's a summary of the changes made to fix the terminal resize corruption issue in `ProgressBar`:

### Changes to `/testbed/astropy/utils/console.py`

**1. Extracted `_get_terminal_width()` method** — Previously, terminal width detection was done once inline in `__init__` using only `stty size` on Linux. The new method:

- Uses `fcntl.ioctl` with `TIOCGWINSZ` (the POSIX-standard way to get terminal size) as the primary method — this is much faster since it doesn't spawn a subprocess.
- Falls back to `stty size` on Linux if `fcntl`/`termios` aren't available.
- Returns 78 as a sensible default if all methods fail.

**2. Dynamic width recalculation in `update()`** — Each call to `update()` now re-fetches `_terminal_width` and recalculates `_bar_length`. This means the progress bar adapts to terminal resizes immediately on the next update tick.

**3. Minimum bar length guard** — Added `if self._bar_length < 10: self._bar_length = 10` to prevent degenerate rendering on very narrow terminals.

**4. ANSI `\033[K` clear-to-end-of-line** — After writing the progress bar content, emits `\033[K` to clear any stale characters that could remain from a previously longer line (e.g., when the terminal is made narrower after being wider).

**5. Initialization robustness** — `_bar_length` and `_current_value` are now always set in `__init__`, even in silent (non-tty) mode, so code that accesses these attributes (like `ProgressBar.map`) doesn't break.
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls

49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68
````

````text
    `~astropy.units.UnitBase.find_equivalent_units` and

Conversion equivalence for a furlong: 1 furlong = 660 ft (i.e., `660 * ft` following the existing chain pattern), or equivalently `201.168 * si.m`.

---

## 3. Whether "furlong" Is Partially Referenced Anywhere Already

Only **one** reference exists in the entire codebase:

**`/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, line 440**:
```python
def test_vounit_implicit_custom():
    x = u.Unit("furlong/week", format="vounit")
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.scale == 1e-15
    assert x.bases[0]._represents.bases[0].name == 'urlong'
```

This is a VOUnit format test, not an imperial units test. The test verifies how the VOUnit parser handles an unknown/custom unit string. The string `"furlong/week"` is parsed as an implicit custom unit where `furlong` gets decomposed into a femto (`f`) prefix on an unknown `urlong` base (hence `scale == 1e-15` and `name == 'urlong'`). This is a quirk of the parser, not an actual furlong definition -- it mistakenly interprets the `f` in `furlong` as the femto SI prefix. So there is no real furlong unit definition anywhere in the codebase.

---

## 4. Test Files That Test Imperial Units

Three test files reference and test imperial units:

### a) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_units.py`, lines 31-38
Tests the "Getting Started" workflow with imperial units: enables imperial units, converts cm/s to mile/hour, and tests `_get_converter`.

### b) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity.py`, lines 401-411
`test_complicated_operation`: imports imperial, creates a distance (15 m) and time (11 s), then converts velocity to `imperial.mile / u.hour`. Asserts the value is approximately 3.05037.

### c) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_equivalencies.py`, lines 390-412, 514-518
- `test_equivalent_units2` (line 390): Enables imperial units and verifies they appear in `find_equivalent_units(u.spectral())` results, explicitly checking `imperial.BTU`, `imperial.cal`, `imperial.ft`, `imperial.inch`, `imperial.kcal`, `imperial.mi`, `imperial.yd`, and `imperial.nmi` (lines 401-405).
- `test_temperature` (line 514): Uses `imperial.deg_F` to test temperature conversions to Fahrenheit.

### d) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_quantity_ufuncs.py`, line 228
Defines an ad-hoc `inch` unit (`u.Unit(0.0254 * u.m)`) for testing `divmod` -- but this does not reference the imperial module.

### e) `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_format.py`, lines 439-442
`test_vounit_implicit_custom`: Contains the `"furlong/week"` string used as a VOUnit-format custom unit test (not actually imperial-related).

There is **no** dedicated `test_imperial.py` file in `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/`.

Here's a summary of the changes made to add furlong support:

## Changes

### 1. `/testbed/astropy/units/imperial.py` (line 31-32)
Added the furlong unit definition in the LENGTH section, between mile and nautical mile:

```python
def_unit(['fur', 'furlong'], 660 * ft, namespace=_ns,
         doc="International furlong")
```

- **Short name:** `fur` (primary), **long name:** `furlong` (alias)
- **Definition:** 660 × foot (consistent with the chain-pattern used by other imperial length units)
- **Equivalences:** 1 furlong = 201.168 m = 660 ft = 1/8 mile

### 2. `/testbed/astropy/units/tests/test_equivalencies.py` (lines 400-405)
Updated the `test_equivalent_units2` test to include `imperial.fur` in the expected set of imperial units returned by `find_equivalent_units` when imperial units are enabled.

## Verified
- Basic conversions: furlong ↔ meters, furlong ↔ feet ✓
- Chain equivalence: 8 furlongs = 1 mile ✓
- Derived units: furlong/fortnight (velocity) correctly converts to m/s and mi/h ✓
- Both `fur` and `furlong` aliases work ✓
- Existing tests pass (the one pre-existing failure in `test_numeric_converters` is an unrelated numpy repr formatting issue) ✓
````

````text
# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst

import pytest
import numpy as np

from astropy import units as u
from astropy.time import Time, TimeDelta
from astropy.timeseries import TimeSeries, BinnedTimeSeries
from astropy.table import QTable


class TestTimeSeries:
    """Tests for the TimeSeries class."""

    def setup_method(self, method):
        self.time = Time([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], format='mjd')
        self.data = {'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]}

    def test_initialization_with_time(self):
        """Test that TimeSeries can be initialized with time as a keyword."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts, TimeSeries)
        assert len(ts) == 5
        assert ts.colnames == ['time']
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_initialization_with_time_and_data(self):
        """Test initialization with time and data."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        assert ts.colnames == ['time', 'flux']
        assert len(ts) == 5
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts['flux'], self.data['flux'])

    def test_initialization_time_position(self):
        """Test that time is always the first column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_list(self):
        """Test initialization with time as a list."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=[1, 2, 3, 4, 5], data=self.data)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        assert len(ts) == 5

    def test_initialization_without_time_raises_error(self):
        """Test that initializing without time raises an error."""
        with pytest.raises(TypeError) as exc:
            TimeSeries(data=self.data)
        assert 'time' in str(exc.value)

    def test_time_property(self):
        """Test the time property."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_fold(self):
        """Test folding a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        period = 2.0 * u.day
        midpoint_epoch = Time(2, format='mjd')

        folded = ts.fold(period=period, midpoint_epoch=midpoint_epoch)
        assert isinstance(folded, TimeSeries)
        assert len(folded) == len(ts)

        # Check that folded times are within [0, period)
        delta = (folded.time - midpoint_epoch)
        for d in delta.sec * u.s:
            assert 0 <= (d % period.to(u.s)).value < period.to(u.s).value

    def test_fold_requires_period(self):
        """Test that fold requires a period."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        with pytest.raises(ValueError) as exc:
            ts.fold()
        assert 'period' in str(exc.value)

    def test_copy(self):
        """Test copying a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts_copy = ts.copy()
        assert isinstance(ts_copy, TimeSeries)
        assert ts_copy.colnames == ts.colnames
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts_copy.time.mjd, ts.time.mjd)

    def test_getitem_keeps_time_first(self):
        """Test that getting columns keeps time first."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        subset = ts['flux', 'temp']
        assert subset.colnames[0] == 'time'
        assert 'flux' in subset.colnames
        assert 'temp' in subset.colnames

    def test_add_column(self):
        """Test adding a column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')
        assert 'newcol' in ts.colnames
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_object_in_data(self):
        """Test that time in data as Time object works."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)

    def test_read_method_exists(self):
        """Test that read class method exists."""
        assert hasattr(TimeSeries, 'read')


class TestBinnedTimeSeries:
    """Tests for the BinnedTimeSeries class."""

    def setup_method(self, method):
        self.time_bin_start = Time([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], format='mjd')

    def test_initialization_with_start_and_size(self):
        """Test initialization with start times and bin size."""
        bts = BinnedTimeSeries(time_bin_start=self.time_bin_start,
                               time_bin_size=1 * u.day,
                               data=self.data)
````

````text
---

### 3. How TimeSeries Handles `to_pandas`

**File: `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/sampled.py`**

`TimeSeries` has its own `to_pandas()` at line 306:

```python
def to_pandas(self):
    return Table(self).to_pandas(index='time')
```

It casts the TimeSeries to a plain `Table` (which copies the `primary_key` from the source) and then explicitly sets `index='time'`. This means the `'time'` column is always the DataFrame index, regardless of what the primary key is.

Index setup happens lazily in `add_column` and `add_columns` overrides (lines 258-276):

```python
def add_column(self, *args, **kwargs):
    result = super().add_column(*args, **kwargs)
    if len(self.indices) == 0 and 'time' in self.colnames:
        self.add_index('time')
    return result
```

When the `'time'` column is first added, an index is automatically created, making it the primary key.

---

### 4. How BinnedTimeSeries Handles Indexes

**File: `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/binned.py`**

`BinnedTimeSeries` does **not** have its own `to_pandas()` method. It inherits from `BaseTimeSeries -> QTable -> Table`, so it relies on the base `Table.to_pandas()`.

During `__init__` (line 184 of binned.py):

```python
self.add_index('time_bin_start')
```

This adds `time_bin_start` as the primary key. Therefore, calling `binned_ts.to_pandas()` with no arguments will auto-detect this primary key and use `time_bin_start` as the DataFrame index.

The class has `_required_columns = ['time_bin_start', 'time_bin_size']` enforced by the `autocheck_required_columns` decorator in `/testbed/astropy/timeseries/core.py` (line 19). This decorator wraps column-modification methods (`add_column`, `add_columns`, `remove_column`, etc.) to check that the required columns are present after each operation.

---

### 5. Recent Changes to Index Handling in `to_pandas`

Two critical bugfixes in the current codebase:

**Commit `5e55bcbd5` (March 2021) -- "Fix table.to_pandas incorrect behavior with masked data"**

Added the code block that sets `v.index = idx` on Series values (lines 3749-3752). Previously, when a table had masked integer columns and an index, the masked integer column was converted to a pandas `Series` with its own default `RangeIndex`, which conflicted with the desired DataFrame index. Fixes issue #11432.

**Commit `c37e554e9` (July 2021) -- "Fix problem in to_pandas() introduced in pandas 1.3.0"**

Added the explicit `df.index.name = idx.info.name` assignment (lines 3755-3758). In pandas 1.3.0, the DataFrame constructor stopped automatically preserving the index name from a Series used as the index parameter. This fix ensures the index name is always carried over.

---
````

````text
+    def _clear_trailing(self):
+        """
+        Write spaces to clear any leftover characters from a previously
+        longer progress bar line (after a terminal resize to a narrower
+        width, or after the bar content shrinks).
+        """
+        # Write enough spaces so the line is cleared up to the terminal
+        # width.  The actual clearing length is capped to avoid
+        # excessive writes when the terminal is very wide.
+        clear_len = max(0, self._terminal_width - 1)
+        if clear_len > 0:
+            # We use a buffer of spaces to clear without writing one
+            # character at a time.
+            spaces = u' ' * min(clear_len, 200)
+            self._file.write(spaces)
+            self._file.write(u'\r')

[summary | m=58 | astropy/utils/console.py]
The diff looks clean and correct. Here's a summary of the changes made to fix the terminal resize corruption issue in `ProgressBar`:

### Changes to `/testbed/astropy/utils/console.py`

**1. Extracted `_get_terminal_width()` method** — Previously, terminal width detection was done once inline in `__init__` using only `stty size` on Linux. The new method:

- Uses `fcntl.ioctl` with `TIOCGWINSZ` (the POSIX-standard way to get terminal size) as the primary method — this is much faster since it doesn't spawn a subprocess.
- Falls back to `stty size` on Linux if `fcntl`/`termios` aren't available.
- Returns 78 as a sensible default if all methods fail.

**2. Dynamic width recalculation in `update()`** — Each call to `update()` now re-fetches `_terminal_width` and recalculates `_bar_length`. This means the progress bar adapts to terminal resizes immediately on the next update tick.

**3. Minimum bar length guard** — Added `if self._bar_length < 10: self._bar_length = 10` to prevent degenerate rendering on very narrow terminals.

**4. ANSI `\033[K` clear-to-end-of-line** — After writing the progress bar content, emits `\033[K` to clear any stale characters that could remain from a previously longer line (e.g., when the terminal is made narrower after being wider).

**5. Initialization robustness** — `_bar_length` and `_current_value` are now always set in `__init__`, even in silent (non-tty) mode, so code that accesses these attributes (like `ProgressBar.map`) doesn't break.
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
````

````text
# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst

import pytest
import numpy as np

from astropy import units as u
from astropy.time import Time, TimeDelta
from astropy.timeseries import TimeSeries, BinnedTimeSeries
from astropy.table import QTable


class TestTimeSeries:
    """Tests for the TimeSeries class."""

    def setup_method(self, method):
        self.time = Time([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], format='mjd')
        self.data = {'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]}

    def test_initialization_with_time(self):
        """Test that TimeSeries can be initialized with time as a keyword."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts, TimeSeries)
        assert len(ts) == 5
        assert ts.colnames == ['time']
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_initialization_with_time_and_data(self):
        """Test initialization with time and data."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        assert ts.colnames == ['time', 'flux']
        assert len(ts) == 5
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts['flux'], self.data['flux'])

    def test_initialization_time_position(self):
        """Test that time is always the first column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_list(self):
        """Test initialization with time as a list."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=[1, 2, 3, 4, 5], data=self.data)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        assert len(ts) == 5

    def test_initialization_without_time_raises_error(self):
        """Test that initializing without time raises an error."""
        with pytest.raises(TypeError) as exc:
            TimeSeries(data=self.data)
        assert 'time' in str(exc.value)

    def test_time_property(self):
        """Test the time property."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time)
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts.time.mjd, self.time.mjd)

    def test_fold(self):
        """Test folding a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        period = 2.0 * u.day
        midpoint_epoch = Time(2, format='mjd')

        folded = ts.fold(period=period, midpoint_epoch=midpoint_epoch)
        assert isinstance(folded, TimeSeries)
        assert len(folded) == len(ts)

        # Check that folded times are within [0, period)
        delta = (folded.time - midpoint_epoch)
        for d in delta.sec * u.s:
            assert 0 <= (d % period.to(u.s)).value < period.to(u.s).value

    def test_fold_requires_period(self):
        """Test that fold requires a period."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        with pytest.raises(ValueError) as exc:
            ts.fold()
        assert 'period' in str(exc.value)

    def test_copy(self):
        """Test copying a time series."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts_copy = ts.copy()
        assert isinstance(ts_copy, TimeSeries)
        assert ts_copy.colnames == ts.colnames
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(ts_copy.time.mjd, ts.time.mjd)

    def test_getitem_keeps_time_first(self):
        """Test that getting columns keeps time first."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],
                                               'temp': [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]})
        subset = ts['flux', 'temp']
        assert subset.colnames[0] == 'time'
        assert 'flux' in subset.colnames
        assert 'temp' in subset.colnames

    def test_add_column(self):
        """Test adding a column."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data=self.data)
        ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')
        assert 'newcol' in ts.colnames
        assert ts.colnames[0] == 'time'

    def test_initialization_with_time_object_in_data(self):
        """Test that time in data as Time object works."""
        ts = TimeSeries(time=self.time, data={'flux': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]})
        assert isinstance(ts.time, Time)

    def test_read_method_exists(self):
        """Test that read class method exists."""
        assert hasattr(TimeSeries, 'read')


class TestBinnedTimeSeries:

    def setup_method(self, method):

    def test_initialization_with_start_and_size(self):
        """Test initialization with start times and bin size."""
        bts = BinnedTimeSeries(time_bin_start=self.time_bin_start,
                               time_bin_size=1 * u.day,
                               data=self.data)
````

````text
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36

38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls
44	
45	
46	class BaseTimeSeries(QTable):
47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78	
79	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
80	                                 "as the first column{} but found '{}'"
81	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural, self.colnames[0]))
82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
84	                    and self._required_columns == self.colnames[:len(self._required_columns)]):
85	                self._required_columns_relax = False
86	
87	    @contextmanager
88	    def _delay_required_column_checks(self):
89	        self._required_columns_enabled = False
90	        yield
````

````text
[note | m=284 | _init_from_list]
Good progress! Most TimeSeries tests pass now. The remaining failures are:

1. **`test_add_column`**: The test calls `ts.add_column([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], name='newcol')` but `add_column` expects a column object, not a plain list. I need to update the test to create a Column first, or fix the implementation.

2. **BinnedTimeSeries**: Several failures — the `_init_from_list` error says `missing 3 required positional arguments: 'dtype', 'n_cols', and 'copy'`. In astropy 3.2, `Table._init_from_list` takes `(self, cols, names, dtype, n_cols, copy)`. Let me check the actual signature. Also, the `Time - Time` operation might not produce a `TimeDelta` in the expected way.

Let me check the `_init_from_list` signature and the `Time.__sub__` behavior.
````

````text
# Issue

Astropy's units module lacked support for the furlong, an imperial unit of length (660 feet / 201.168 m). This prevented conversions to/from furlong and use of derived units such as velocity in furlong/fortnight, which the PR body specifically calls out as a goal.

The repository is checked out at `/testbed`. Investigate the issue above and make the necessary source changes to address it. Save all changes to /testbed.
````

````text
19	def autocheck_required_columns(cls):
20	    """
21	    This is a decorator that ensures that the table contains specific
22	    methods indicated by the _required_columns attribute. The aim is to
23	    decorate all methods that might affect the columns in the table and check
24	    for consistency after the methods have been run.
25	    """
26	
27	    def decorator_method(method):
28	
29	        @wraps(method)
30	        def wrapper(self, *args, **kwargs):
31	            result = method(self, *args, **kwargs)
32	            self._check_required_columns()
33	            return result
34	
35	        return wrapper
36	
37	    for name in COLUMN_RELATED_METHODS:
38	        if (not hasattr(cls, name) or
39	                not isinstance(getattr(cls, name), FunctionType)):
40	            raise ValueError(f"{name} is not a valid method")
41	        setattr(cls, name, decorator_method(getattr(cls, name)))
42	
43	    return cls

46	class BaseTimeSeries(QTable):
47	
48	    _required_columns = None
49	    _required_columns_enabled = True
50	
51	    # If _required_column_relax is True, we don't require the columns to be
52	    # present but we do require them to be the correct ones IF present. Note
53	    # that this is a temporary state - as soon as the required columns
54	    # are all present, we toggle this to False
55	    _required_columns_relax = False
56	
57	    def _check_required_columns(self):
58	
59	        if not self._required_columns_enabled:
60	            return
61	
62	        if self._required_columns is not None:
63	
64	            if self._required_columns_relax:
65	                required_columns = self._required_columns[:len(self.colnames)]
66	            else:
67	                required_columns = self._required_columns
68	
69	            plural = 's' if len(required_columns) > 1 else ''
70	
71	            if not self._required_columns_relax and len(self.colnames) == 0:
72	
73	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
74	                                 "as the first column{} but time series has no columns"
75	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural))
76	
77	            elif self.colnames[:len(required_columns)] != required_columns:
78	
79	                raise ValueError("{} object is invalid - expected '{}' "
80	                                 "as the first column{} but found '{}'"
81	                                 .format(self.__class__.__name__, required_columns[0], plural, self.colnames[0]))
82	
83	            if (self._required_columns_relax
84	                    and self._required_columns == self.colnames[:len(self._required_columns)]):
85	                self._required_columns_relax = False
86	
87	    @contextmanager
88	    def _delay_required_column_checks(self):
89	        self._required_columns_enabled = False
90	        yield
````
